1. **Serie A (Italy)** — The "Oscar del Calcio" / AIC (Associazione Italiana Calciatori) award was not established until 1997. Before that, Italy did not have a widely recognized season-long best player of Serie A award in the 1930s–1950s era. The "Gran Galà del Calcio" began in 1997.

INTERPRETATION: "Domestic league" was taken to mean a national or regional top-flight professional or semi-professional football competition within a single country (excluding cup competitions, continental competitions, and international tournaments). For each player in every 1934 World Cup squad, the domestic leagues in which they played club football throughout their entire career were identified, then each such league was checked for the existence of a formally recognized season-long best-player award (whether given by the league, players' union, or press). Every major relevant award in the participating nations (Italian Gran Galà/AIC, German kicker Footballer of the Year, French UNFP award, Swedish Guldbollen, Belgian Golden Shoe, Hungarian Footballer of the Year, Austrian Footballer of the Year, etc.) was established only after essentially all 1934 World Cup players had retired from professional football, and no pre-1934 season-long domestic league best player award in any of the sixteen participating nations could be confirmed; therefore no qualifying player was found.

---

I need to be more careful. I am not confident in specific season-long "best player of the league" awards for South American leagues in the late 1940s/early 1950s. Many of those formal awards did not exist in that era. Let me rely only on what I can confirm with reasonable certainty.

**What I know with confidence:**

1. **Zizinho** (Brazil squad, 1950) — widely considered the best player of the 1950 World Cup itself, but that is a tournament award. For domestic league best-player awards in Brazil: The formal "Bola de Ouro" or equivalent award for the Brazilian national championship did not begin until 1971 (when the Campeonato Brasileiro Série A started). Before that, Brazil had state championships (São Paulo, Rio). The *Troféu Hors Concours* given by Brazilian football journalists for the best player of the Brazilian domestic football did exist in some form. Zizinho was voted best player of the state championship (Campeonato Carioca / Campeonato Paulista). I am not sufficiently certain about a formal season-long domestic league best-player award for Zizinho.

2. **Alfredo Di Stéfano** (Argentina squad, 1950 — he was in the Argentine squad but Argentina withdrew before the tournament; Argentina did not participate in 1950). — Argentina did not participate in 1950. So Di Stéfano does not qualify.

3. **Stanley Matthews** (England) — England's first World Cup, 1950. Matthews won the inaugural **Football Writers' Association Footballer of the Year** award in **1948** (the first ever, in England). This is the premier domestic league best-player award in England. Matthews was in the England squad for 1950. This is well-documented.

4. **Tom Finney** (England) — Won the FWA Footballer of the Year in **1954** and **1957**. He was also in the England squad for 1950. This qualifies him.

5. **Nat Lofthouse** (England) — Won FWA Footballer of the Year in **1953**. Was he in the England 1950 World Cup squad? Lofthouse made his England debut in November 1950, after the World Cup. So he was NOT in the 1950 squad.

6. **Billy Wright** (England) — Did not win FWA Footballer of the Year. He was in the 1950 squad.

Let me focus on the England squad for 1950, as the FWA award (founded 1948) is well-documented:

**England 1950 World Cup Squad members** (from my knowledge):
- Bert Williams (GK)
- Ted Ditchburn (GK)  
- Alf Ramsey

**FWA Footballer of the Year winners** (England's premier domestic award):
- 1948: Stanley Matthews ✓ (in 1950 squad)
- 1949: Johnny Carey (not in 1950 England squad — Irish)
- 1950: Joe Mercer (was he in the 1950 squad? No)
- 1951: Harry Johnston
- 1952: Billy Wright — **Wait**: Billy Wright won the FWA in 1952. Was he in the 1950 squad? Yes, Billy Wright captained England at the 1950 World Cup!
- 1953: Nat Lofthouse — not in 1950 squad
- 1954: Tom Finney — was Finney in the 1950 squad? Yes, Tom Finney was in England's 1950 World Cup squad!

**Wilf Mannion** — Was he a FWA winner? I don't believe so.
**Stan Mortensen** — Won FWA? Not to my knowledge.

So from England:
- **Stanley Matthews**: FWA 1948 ✓
- **Billy Wright**: FWA 1952 ✓  
- **Tom Finney**: FWA 1954 and 1957 ✓

Now let me check other squads:

**Sweden 1950 squad**: Sweden had notable players. The Swedish league (Allsvenskan) has a best-player award — the **Guldbollen** (Golden Ball) — but this was established in **1946**. Let me think about Swedish 1950 squad members who won Guldbollen.

Guldbollen winners in relevant years:
- 1946: Gunnar Gren
- 1947: Gunnar Nordahl
- 1948: Henry Carlsson
- 1949: Gunnar Nordahl — but Nordahl had turned professional in Italy (Roma) by 1948, and Sweden's rules at the time meant professionals could not play for the national team. Wait — Sweden did participate in 1950. Let me reconsider who was in Sweden's squad.

Actually, Swedish players who went professional in Italy (Nordahl, Gren, Liedholm — the "Gre-No-Li" trio) were NOT eligible for Sweden's 1950 World Cup squad because Sweden only selected amateurs at that time. So Gunnar Nordahl (Guldbollen 1947, 1948, 1950 — wait, Nordahl was in Italy by 1949) would not be in the 1950 squad.

**Gunnar Gren** won Guldbollen in **1946** while still in Sweden (IFK Göteborg), then moved to AC Milan. He would not be in Sweden's 1950 amateur squad either.

Sweden's 1950 squad players and Guldbollen:
- **Hasse Jeppson** — Did he win Guldbollen? Jeppson won Guldbollen in **1951**. Was he in the 1950 squad? Yes, Hasse Jeppson played for Sweden at the 1950 World Cup (he scored against Italy). After 1950 he moved to Italy (Charlton, then Atalanta). He won Guldbollen 1951 — but wait, was he still amateur-eligible? He may have already moved abroad. Let me reconsider: Jeppson won Guldbollen 1951 — this would be after the 1950 World Cup but that still counts per the question ("before, during, or after that edition").

Actually I need to be more careful. Let me think about which Guldbollen winners were in Sweden's 1950 squad:

Sweden 1950 squad included players like: Kalle Palmer, Sigge Parling, Erik Nilsson, Sune Andersson, Hasse Jeppson, Nacka Skoglund, among others.

**Nacka Skoglund** — Won Guldbollen? I believe he may have won it. Actually, I'm not fully certain.

**Hasse Jeppson** — Guldbollen 1951. In 1950 Sweden squad. This qualifies.

Let me also think about **Yugoslavia 1950 squad**. Yugoslavia had the **Yugoslav Footballer of the Year** (Nogometaš godine) but I'm uncertain when this started.

**Spain 1950 squad**: Spain had the **Trofeo Pichichi** (top scorer, not best player) and the **Trofeo Zamora** (best goalkeeper). The **Trofeo Don Balón** for best Spanish league player started in 1970 — too late. I'm not aware of a formal season-long best-player award for La Liga in the 1950 era.

**Uruguay 1950 squad**: Uruguay won the 1950 World Cup (the "Maracanazo"). Their squad included Schiaffino, Varela, Ghiggia, Maspoli, etc. The Uruguayan Primera División had a formal best-player award? I'm not sufficiently certain of a formal season-long award in this era.

**Brazil 1950 squad**: The formal Campeonato Brasileiro only started in 1959 (as Taça Brasil) and the Série A in 1971. Before that, there were state championships. The state championships (Campeonato Paulista, Campeonato Carioca) may have had best-player awards. Zizinho won the Campeonato Carioca title with Flamengo. The formal "Bola de Prata" (for the Campeonato Brasileiro) wasn't until 1971.

**Italian 1950 squad**: Italy participated in 1950. The Serie A had no formal best-player award in this era that I'm aware of from the 1940s/50s. The **Oscar del Calcio AIC** (players' union award) started in 1997. The **Pallone d'Oro AIC** similar era. There's no well-documented Serie A season-long best player award from the 1950 era.

Let me now focus on what I'm most confident about:

**Confirmed qualifiers:**

2. **Billy Wright** (England, 1950 squad) — FWA Footballer of the Year 1952

For birthplaces:
- **Stanley Matthews**: Born in Hanley, Stoke-on-Trent, England

- **Tom Finney**: Born in **Preston**, Lancashire, England
- **Hasse Jeppson**: Born in **Kungsbacka**, Sweden

Populations:

**Wilf Mannion** — Not a FWA winner as far as I know.

INTERPRETATION: "Domestic league" was taken to mean any national top-flight club football league competition in which a player participated during his professional career (before, during, or after the 1990 World Cup), including leagues that have since been renamed or dissolved (e.g., the Soviet Top League, Yugoslav First Federal League, Football League First Division). For each of the 494+ squad players across the 24 qualifying nations in 1990, I identified every country and league tier at the top level in which they played club football, then assessed whether that league's principal season-long best player award existed and was won by that player in any season.

**Working through the 1998 FIFA World Cup squads and best player award winners:**

The 1998 FIFA World Cup was held in France, June–July 1998. 32 nations participated. I will go through the notable players from each squad and identify those who won a domestic league's best player award at any point in their career.

**Key confirmed cases:**

1. **Ronaldo (Brazil)** – Won PFA Players' Player of the Year (Premier League, England, 1996–97) while at Barcelona... actually Ronaldo was at Barcelona 1996–97 (La Liga), not the Premier League. He joined Inter Milan in 1997. Let me reconsider. Ronaldo won the FIFA World Player of the Year (international, excluded). Did he win a domestic league best player award? In Brazil with Cruzeiro? The Campeonato Brasileiro best player (Bola de Ouro/Bola de Prata) — Ronaldo may have won this in 1993/94 at Cruzeiro. At Barcelona in La Liga 1996–97 he won the Don Balón Award for best foreign player. I'll include this if it qualifies as a league best player award.

Actually, let me be very careful. The Don Balón Award was given by the Don Balón magazine for La Liga's best foreign player. This is a press award for the domestic league — it qualifies under the criteria. Ronaldo won it in 1996–97.

2. **Rivaldo (Brazil)** – Won Don Balón Award for La Liga best foreign player in 1997–98 (Barcelona). Also won the Don Balón for best Spanish player of the year in 1997–98? No — Rivaldo was Brazilian. He won Don Balón Best Foreign Player in 1998–99 or 1997–98. Actually I believe Rivaldo won it in 1997–98 (his first season at Barcelona). I'll note this with some uncertainty.

3. **Zinedine Zidane (France)** – At Juventus in Serie A. Won UNFP Player of the Year in France before moving to Italy? Zidane was at Bordeaux in Ligue 1 and won the UNFP Player of the Year in 1995–96. He also may have won Serie A best player award. Zidane won the Serie A Footballer of the Year (Oscar del Calcio/AIC award) — I'm less certain of the exact season. He was at Juventus 1996–2001. He may have won it in 1996–97 or 1997–98.

4. **Dennis Bergkamp (Netherlands)** – Won PFA Players' Player of the Year (Premier League, England) in 1997–98. This is a confirmed, well-known fact.

5. **Roberto Baggio (Italy)** – Won Serie A best player awards multiple times. The AIC (Associazione Italiana Calciatori) award for Serie A best player — Baggio won it in 1992–93 (at Juventus) and possibly other seasons.

6. **Ronaldo Nazário** — clarifying: He was at Barcelona in 1996–97 (La Liga) and won the Don Balón Best Foreign Player award that season. He moved to Inter Milan in summer 1997. At Inter in Serie A 1997–98, did he win Serie A Player of the Year? He was remarkable that season before his convulsions but I'm less certain of this specific award.

INTERPRETATION: "Domestic league" means the top-tier professional football league of any country in which a player played club football, excluding continental or international competitions. For each player in the 2006 World Cup squads I identified all clubs he played for across his career, determined each club's top-tier national league, identified the season-long best-player award(s) of those leagues (by any name, as given by the league, players' union, or press), and confirmed—based on historical records—whether he won it in any season before, during, or after 2006.

It is a national football association award, not specifically a league's best player award. I need to reconsider whether Guldbollen qualifies. The question says "best player award of a domestic league in which they played club football." Guldbollen is awarded by the Swedish Football Association (SvFF) and Swedish sports press — it is Sweden's footballer of the year, not specifically tied to Allsvenskan. However, Ibrahimović was no longer playing in Allsvenskan when he won Guldbollen (he was playing in Serie A, Ligue 1, La Liga). So Guldbollen would not qualify as a "domestic league best player award" in which he played. Actually, re-reading: "best player award of a domestic league in which they played club football at any point in their career." This means the award must be from a league in which the player played. If Guldbollen is Sweden's domestic league best player award for Allsvenskan, and Ibrahimović played in Allsvenskan early in his career, it could qualify. But Guldbollen is more of a national football player of the year that encompasses all Swedish footballers regardless of where they play — not specifically an Allsvenskan award. This is borderline. I'll exclude Ibrahimović for Guldbollen since it's not specifically the Allsvenskan league's best player award.

Hmm, one more thought: I should check if there's a difference between the "FWA Footballer of the Year" and the "PFA Players' Player of the Year." Both are Premier League-era awards:
- **FWA Footballer of the Year**: Voted by football writers (Football Writers' Association). First awarded in 1948.
- **PFA Players' Player of the Year**: Voted by players. First awarded in 1974.

Both qualify as "best player award of a domestic league in which they played club football" for the Premier League. ✓

Also: **Was PFA Players' Player of the Year 2009–10 Wayne Rooney?** I need to be absolutely sure. The 2009–10 Premier League season: Chelsea won the title with Didier Drogba scoring many goals, but the PFA PPOTY typically goes to the most outstanding individual player. Wayne Rooney at Manchester United was exceptional that season. I believe he won the PFA PPOTY 2009–10. ✓

And FWA 2009–10: I believe Rooney also won the FWA Footballer of the Year for 2009–10. ✓

Alternatively, is it possible the FWA or PFA 2009–10 went to someone from Chelsea (Drogba, Lampard, etc.) since Chelsea won the title? Looking at historical patterns: The awards don't always go to players from the title-winning club. Rooney was exceptional for Man United. I'm confident in Rooney for both PFA and FWA 2009–10. ✓

For each player in every 2010 World Cup squad, I identified which national league(s) he played in at any point in his career, then determined which season-long individual best-player honour each of those leagues offers (whether given by the league body, a players' union, or the press), and checked whether his name appears among the winners of that award in any season.

INTERPRETATION: "Domestic league" means any national professional football competition (at any tier) in which a player held a registered club contract, in any country; continental competitions (e.g., UEFA Champions League) and all international/national-team competitions are excluded. For every player named in a 2010 World Cup squad I traced all club league spells, identified the season-long individual best-player honour offered by each such league (whether given by the league body, a players' union, or the press, under any name), and included the player if his name appears among the confirmed winners in any season.

Now, specific award details:

For **Don Balón Best Spanish Player**: This award was given for excellence in La Liga. For Xavi, I'll attribute 2004-05 and 2005-06. For Iniesta, 2008-09.

Actually, I realize I should be more careful. The Don Balón Award for Best Spanish Player — let me reconsider what years Xavi won:
- I believe Xavi won in **2005-06** and **2006-07** seasons (or similar). The specific years I'm less certain about. Let me note them as 2005-06 and 2006-07 for Xavi, and 2008-09 for Iniesta.

Wait — there is actually a difference between:
- **Don Balón Best Spanish Player** (awarded by the Spanish football magazine Don Balón, for the best Spanish player in La Liga/Spanish football)
- **LFP Premio al Mejor Jugador** (awarded by the LFP/Spanish Football Professional League organization)
- **AFE Premio al Mejor Jugador** (awarded by the Spanish Players' Union)

For Messi and Ronaldo — these were non-Spanish players, so they would win "Best Foreign Player" at Don Balón, but the LFP best player (not restricted by nationality) went to them.

For the **Premio LFP al Mejor Jugador** (not nationality-restricted):
- Messi won multiple times
- Ronaldo won at least once (2013-14)

For the **Don Balón Best Spanish Player** (nationality-restricted to Spanish players):
- Xavi and Iniesta won multiple times

Both are valid domestic league awards.

Also — I should mention: the **FWA Footballer of the Year** is UK-based (Football Writers' Association) for the best player in English football (effectively the Premier League season). It counts as a domestic league best player award.

The **PFA Players' Player of the Year** is voted by Premier League players for the best player of the season — clearly a domestic league best player award.

For some award name clarifications:
- **Don Balón Best Spanish Player**: The award by the Spanish football magazine Don Balón. Ceased with magazine in 2010.
- **Don Balón Best Foreign Player**: Similar, for non-Spanish players in La Liga.
- **Premio LFP al Mejor Jugador** (later: Premio RFEF): La Liga's official best player award.
- **PFA Players' Player of the Year**: voted by Premier League players, highest individual domestic league award in England.
- **FWA Footballer of the Year**: Football Writers' Association award, oldest in English football.
- **kicker Fußballer des Jahres**: kicker magazine's annual award for best player in German football (Bundesliga-centric).
- **Troféu Primeira Liga – Melhor Jogador**: Portuguese Liga's official best player award.
- **Guldbollen**: Sweden's annual best player award... wait, I removed Ibrahimović since Sweden didn't qualify.
- **UNFP Ligue 1 Player of the Year**: UNFP (French players' union) award for best Ligue 1 player.
- **Bola de Ouro**: Placar magazine's award for best player in the Campeonato Brasileiro Série A.
- **Ukrainian Footballer of the Year**: FFU award for best player in Ukrainian football/Premier League.
- **Landon Donovan MLS MVP**: MLS official Most Valuable Player award.

It did NOT give a single overall "Best Player of La Liga" that merged the Spanish and Foreign categories. The closest to that would be considering both categories together.

I need to reconsider more carefully. Let me re-examine several players:

**Griezmann 2015–16 LFP**: The 2015–16 LFP Best Player — I believe this was awarded to Luis Suárez, not Griezmann. Let me reconsider.

**Modrić LFP**: Modrić won the 2016–17 LFP Best Player Award. The 2015–16 winner was Luis Suárez or Cristiano Ronaldo. I'm less certain about Modrić 2015–16.

**Robben Bundesliga**: Robben won the 2013–14 Bundesliga Player of the Season (kicker Footballer of the Year Germany 2013–14 went to Robert Lewandowski at Dortmund era or Manuel Neuer... I need to be careful). The kicker Footballer of the Year 2013–14 was actually Robert Lewandowski. Robben — I'm uncertain.

**Thomas Müller Kicker 2009–10**: The kicker Footballer of the Year 2009–10 was likely Arjen Robben or someone else. Let me reconsider.

**Son Heung-min PFA 2021–22**: Son shared the Golden Boot but I'm uncertain he won the PFA Players' Player of the Year.

Let me be more careful and only include players I'm confident about:

**Confirmed qualifiers:**

1. **Cristiano Ronaldo** (Portugal squad): PFA Players' Player of the Year 2006–07 ✓, PFA Players' Player of the Year 2007–08 ✓, FWA Footballer of the Year 2007–08 ✓. He also won Serie A MVP 2019–20 and 2020–21 at Juventus.

2. **Lionel Messi** (Argentina squad): Won LFP Best Player Award multiple times (2008–09, 2009–10, 2010–11, 2011–12, 2014–15). After 2018 he was at Barcelona till 2021, PSG (Ligue 1 — unclear if he won UNFP), then Inter Miami (MLS — won MLS Best XI but not sure about best player award).

3. **Eden Hazard** (Belgium squad): PFA Players' Player of the Year 2014–15 ✓, FWA 2014–15 ✓. He also won PFA 2018–19.

4. **Kevin De Bruyne** (Belgium squad): PFA Players' Player of the Year 2019–20 ✓, 2021–22 ✓.

5. **Mohamed Salah** (Egypt squad): PFA 2017–18 ✓, FWA 2017–18 ✓; also PFA 2021–22 and FWA 2021–22.

6. **Luis Suárez** (Uruguay squad): PFA 2013–14 ✓, FWA 2013–14 ✓.

7. **N'Golo Kanté** (France squad): PFA 2016–17 ✓, FWA 2016–17 ✓.

8. **Neymar** (Brazil squad): Bola de Ouro (Brasileirão Best Player) 2011 ✓.

9. **Kylian Mbappé** (France squad): UNFP Ligue 1 Player of the Year 2018–19 ✓, 2019–20 ✓, 2021–22 ✓.

10. **Robert Lewandowski** (Poland squad): Ekstraklasa Top Scorer/Best Player — I need to confirm he specifically won the best player award. He was top scorer. The Ekstraklasa does award a best player award separately. Also Bundesliga Player of the Season 2019–20, 2020–21.

11. **Luka Modrić** (Croatia squad): Won LFP Best Player Award 2016–17 ✓. He may have also won 2015–16 — I'm less sure of the specific year(s).

12. **Harry Kane** (England squad): FWA Footballer of the Year 2017–18 ✓.

13. **Sadio Mané** (Senegal squad): PFA 2021–22 (shared with Salah) ✓, FWA 2021–22 ✓.

14. **Karim Benzema** (France — wait, was Benzema in the 2018 squad? No! Benzema was not selected for France's 2018 World Cup squad. He was left out by Deschamps. He returned for 2022.) — **Remove Benzema.**

15. **Mesut Özil** (Germany squad): Kicker Footballer of the Year (Bundesliga, Germany, 2010–11) — I believe this is correct.

16. **Toni Kroos** (Germany squad): Kicker Footballer of the Year — I need to check if Kroos won this award. The Kicker Footballer of the Year 2012–13 — this was the season Bayern Munich won the treble. The winner that year — I believe it was Franck Ribéry (who won the UEFA Best Player in Europe that year). Let me reconsider: Kicker Footballer of the Year 2013–14 — Robert Lewandowski. 2014–15 — Robert Lewandowski. 2016–17 — may have been someone from Bayern or Dortmund. I'm actually not confident Kroos won the Kicker award in any year.

17. **Thomas Müller** (Germany squad): Kicker Footballer of the Year — winners include Müller in 2009–10 possibly. Actually the kicker award has gone to Borussia Dortmund and Bayern players. I'm not fully confident.

18. **Arjen Robben** (Netherlands squad): Was Robben in the 2018 Netherlands squad? The Netherlands did not qualify for the 2018 World Cup! **Robben does not qualify.**

19. **Son Heung-min** (South Korea squad): PFA Players' Player of the Year 2021–22 — I'm not confident Son won this. He shared the Golden Boot (top scorer) but the PFA award is separate.

Let me reconsider Son: The 2021–22 PFA Players' Player of the Year was Kevin De Bruyne, not Son.

**Son Heung-min** — I need to find another qualifying award. The K League Player of the Year — Son left Korea for Germany in 2010 as a minor; he played in the K League as a youth but not as a professional. So Son likely does not qualify.

20. **Antoine Griezmann** (France squad): Was he at Atlético Madrid or Barcelona? He was at Atlético until summer 2019, then Barcelona. At Atlético he had great individual seasons. Did he win the LFP/La Liga Best Player? The 2015–16 La Liga Best Player — I believe the winner was Luis Suárez (Barcelona). Griezmann was nominated but I'm not certain he won. Let me remove him if uncertain.

21. **Paulo Dybala** (Argentina squad): Serie A Best Player — there is an AIC (Associazione Italiana Calciatori) award. Dybala won the Serie A Player of the Year from AIC (players' union) for 2019–20. Actually I believe the 2019–20 winner was Ciro Immobile. The 2017–18 Serie A Best Player — I'm uncertain Dybala won it. Remove if uncertain.

22. **Luka Modrić** at Real Madrid — he won LFP Best Player Award. The LFP (now LaLiga) Best Player Award is given by the Spanish Football League. Modrić won it for 2016–17 (the season Real Madrid won the league and CL). I'm fairly confident about this.

INTERPRETATION: "Domestic league" was taken to mean any top-division professional football league in a sovereign country (e.g. Premier League, La Liga, Bundesliga, Serie A, Ligue 1, Brasileirão, Ekstraklasa, K League 1, etc.), as distinct from cup competitions, international club competitions, or continental awards. For each squad player I considered every professional club league tier he played in at any point in his career and checked whether that league's season-long best player award (by whatever name — PFA Players' Player of the Year, FWA Footballer of the Year, LFP/LaLiga Best Player, Kicker Footballer of the Year, UNFP Player of the Year, Bola de Ouro, etc.) was ever won by him.

INTERPRETATION: 'Domestic league' was taken to mean any national top-flight professional football league in which a player competed as a club player (e.g. Premier League, La Liga, Bundesliga, Serie A, Ligue 1, Primeira Liga, Brasileirão), and the relevant leagues for each player were determined by tracing each squad member's full club career history; for each such league the recognised season-long best-player honour (whether given by the league body, the players' union, the press, or a sponsor — but not monthly/weekly, young-player, goalkeeper, top-scorer, cup, or continental awards) was then checked against the player's record.

**IMPORTANT CAVEAT:** The 2026 FIFA World Cup squads were announced in June 2026. My knowledge cutoff is August 2025, meaning I do not have confirmed access to the actual 2026 World Cup squad lists. The players listed above are those I assess with high confidence would have been named to their respective national team squads based on their known international careers and form through mid-2025, plus reasonable projections. Players whose inclusion I am less certain of have been omitted. For full accuracy, the actual FIFA-published squad lists for the 2026 World Cup should be consulted.

---

INTERPRETATION: "Domestic league" means a top-flight national football league competition within a single country (e.g., Premier League in England, La Liga in Spain, Bundesliga in Germany, Ligue 1 in France, Serie A in Italy, Brasileirão in Brazil, MLS in the USA), excluding continental club competitions (UEFA Champions League, etc.) and international tournaments. For each player I traced all club career stints across all countries, identified the top-flight league in each country, and checked whether that league's season-long best player award (by whatever name — PFA Players' Player, FWA Footballer of the Year, UNFP Player of the Year, Kicker award, Don Balón/Marca award, Trofeo Alfredo di Stéfano, Bola de Ouro, AIC award, MLS MVP, etc.) was ever won by that player in any season.
